Sweet Potato Casserole

📅 2017-09-18  |  Read Source →

Move aside, other casseroles—sweet potato casserole ranks at the very top of my list when it comes to Thanksgiving sides. I'll be coming back to this recipe time and time again, thanks to its buttery pecan topping. It’s still plenty sweet (thanks to the brown sugar), but not enough to make the sweet potatoes taste like straight-up dessert.

What People Are Saying:“I made this for a Thanksgiving carry in a couple of years ago which was a hit, and just put it in the oven for this years dinner that I’m hosting! Delicioso, my family love this version the best. Thank you!☺️” - dmartin0958214

After preheating the oven and greasing a baking dish with cooking spray, place your sweet potatoes in a large pot and cover with water. Bring them to a boil, then reduce the heat to medium-low and simmer until the potatoes are tender and you can poke them with a fork. Drain the water, then let them cool slightly before transferring to a large bowl. Add the butter to the potatoes, then mash with a potato masher or 2 forks.

Mash until you reach your desired consistency (I prefer it smooth with a few chunks). Once they’ve reached the consistency you’re looking for, add eggs, milk, brown sugar, salt, nutmeg, and vanilla and stir until smooth. Once mixed, transfer to the prepared baking dish.

In a medium bowl, mix all of the topping ingredients together until combined.

Once combined, sprinkle the topping over the sweet potato mixture. Bake the casserole until it is warmed through and the topping is golden brown, then serve immediately while warm.

The full list of ingredients & instructions can be found in the recipe below.

This is a great make-ahead Thanksgiving recipe to save yourself time (and energy) on the big day. Prep the casserole up to step 2, then refrigerate until you're ready to bake (we recommend no more than a few days ahead of time). When you're ready to bake, you can prepare and top with the crumbly topping.

If you have any leftovers (which I can't imagine you would), put them in an airtight container—or cover your casserole dish—and refrigerate for three to five days. This casserole also freezes wonderfully. Similar to prepping it ahead, simply prepare your casserole and bake it without topping it with the pecan topping. Allow it to cool before covering and freezing. When you're ready to serve, simply thaw it in the fridge overnight, top it with the crumbly topping, and bake.

31 Thanksgiving Pies Worth a Second Slice at Breakfast

📅 2017-09-09  |  Read Source →

Some people will happily eat a slice of cheesecake after Thanksgiving dinner. We respect that. But this list of Thanksgiving pie recipes is for those of us who already have our forks poised over the pie table, whipped cream at the ready.

You’ll find the classics: silky pumpkin pie, gooey pecan pie, and apple pie with a double crust and another with cinnamon crumble. Then things get interesting: cranberry meringue with lime-sharpened curd, butterscotch custard with a blanket of toasted marshmallow, a pecan cream pie that doesn’t know when to stop layering. We’ve also got chilled chocolate pies, citrusy custards, and more than a few excellent candidates for breakfast the next morning.

Need more inspiration? Browse our favorite pie recipes or the rest of our Thanksgiving desserts. For help with the pastry, check out our guide to making better pie or the best store-bought pie crusts.

Silky pumpkin custard, a buttery, flaky crust, and just the right amount of warming spice. Maple syrup and vanilla bring extra depth to this Thanksgiving classic, which has converted more than a few pumpkin pie skeptics.

A beautiful lattice crust is just the introduction. Beneath it, Honeycrisp apples meet smoky burnt-sugar caramel and a generous pour of bourbon. Give this pie plenty of time to cool before slicing.

“This old Gramma has been baking pies for MANY years and this is by far the best pecan pie recipe I have ever used,” wrote one of our readers. Gramma from Chattanooga, Tennessee, we salute you.

That crimson cranberry-lime curd is as tart and punchy as it looks; a billowing meringue cap supplies the sweetness. Start the day before serving so the filling has ample time to set.

Claire Saffitz’s deep-dish apple pie builds flavor with reduced cider, vanilla, and cardamom. Sweet-tart Pink Ladies are our first choice for the filling, but plenty of other excellent baking apples work.

If pecan pie and cream pie had a very ambitious child, this would be it. A nut-packed base, brown sugar custard, tangy whipped cream, and candied pecans make each slice a four-layer event.

Detroit’s Sister Pie brings the drama with a flaky crust, dark chocolate ganache, salty-sweet butterscotch custard, and a homemade marshmallow topping toasted to golden peaks. It’s a project, but what a payoff.

This classic Gourmet dessert makes a compelling case for ousting apples at Thanksgiving (jk, obviously you have to make both). Firm-ripe Anjou or Bartlett pears, a little nutmeg, and a flaky lattice crust are all it takes.

Fresh ginger and a trio of warming spices bring depth to the sweet potato custard. The coup de grâce: a cloud of marshmallow-spiked whipped cream that gives sweet potato casserole some dessert-table competition.

The lemon filling means business—it gets the zest of four whole lemons. A towering crown of toasted meringue brings the drama, but you can break up the work over a few days.

For anyone who prefers streusel to a pastry lattice, this is your pie. A thick layer of cinnamon-spiced apples settles beneath a buttery crumb topping. A scoop of vanilla ice cream is highly encouraged.

Alison Roman’s Southern-style custard pie brings lemon zest, tangy buttermilk, and optional saffron to a tender, flaky crust. The saffron adds a delicate floral note and gives the filling its golden glow.

Inspired by the author’s Filipino family holiday traditions, this striking purple pie uses jarred ube halaya and coconut milk for a silky filling. A pearly marshmallow whip makes it look positively regal.

Think giant peanut butter cup + graham cracker crust. A fudgy chocolate layer sits beneath airy peanut butter mousse. This isn’t the time for drippy natural peanut butter; the conventional creamy stuff gives the filling its velvety texture.

Coffee ice cream, bourbon fudge, toasted pecans, and an Oreo crust make a gloriously rich frozen pie. Assemble it up to four days ahead and save that precious oven space for Thanksgiving dinner.

Maple syrup sweetens the warmly spiced pumpkin custard, while a toasty oat-flour crust makes this Gourmet adaptation a welcome gluten-free alternative. Use certified gluten-free oat flour and a labeled gluten-free flour blend.

This pecan pie’s dating app profile would read: “Gooey brown sugar filling, bittersweet chocolate throughout, cocoa crust, fluffy chocolate cream on top.” Are we swiping right? Obviously.

A tart, silky filling and billows of whipped cream make this pie a welcome break from all the brown sugar and spice. An egg yolk in the graham cracker crust helps it hold together for clean slices.

This Pennsylvania Dutch specialty is all about molasses: dark, sticky, and delicious beneath a crumbly topping that bakes into a striking marbled surface. A spoonful of whipped cream makes a fine companion.

What makes this British classic so brilliant? Instead of canned dulce de leche, we make a deep, chewy caramel sauce and add a layer of salted nuts. Bananas and whipped cream take it home.

Inspired by Greek bougatsa, this skillet-baked pie uses rumpled sheets of phyllo instead of traditional pastry. Don’t sweat the tears and folds; they’re what give the cinnamon-scented custard pie its dramatic, shatteringly crisp top.

A salty-sweet peanut-and-graham-cracker crust, banana-packed custard, and a mountain of whipped cream. The peanut topping makes this especially good for anyone who believes banana pudding could use a little crunch.

Imagine pumpkin pie with pecan pie’s best feature: that crunchy, candied nut topping. Now spike the custard with rye whiskey and deepen the crust’s flavor with rye flour. The best of both worlds, indeed.

This Southern specialty folds chocolate chips, bourbon, and your choice of pecans or walnuts into a gooey brown sugar filling. A generous pinch of salt keeps all that sweetness in check.

Before you ask: Yes, it’s dessert. No, there’s no chopped meat in this version. Instead, you get a glorious jumble of dried fruits, apples, warm spices, and rum tucked beneath a golden lattice crust. Prepare the filling at least 24 hours before assembly, or freeze it up to three months ahead.

Cherries at Thanksgiving? Absolutely, when you’re using frozen fruit. Almond flour makes the pastry especially tender, while lime zest brightens the tart cherry filling. A classic double-crust pie worth saving a slice of for breakfast.

23 Healthy Breakfasts That’ll Keep You Full Till Lunch

📅 2017-07-12  |  Read Source →

When it comes to healthy breakfasts, free yourself to eat more than plain yogurt and granola bars. And honestly, those $19 acai bowls from the smoothie shop aren’t that nutritious. A truly healthy breakfast has plenty of carbs for energy (this includes fiber-rich ingredients like whole grains, berries, legumes, and seeds); muscle- and bone-supporting protein; and plenty of healthy fats for nutrient absorption. Many of these recipes can easily be made ahead and doled out from the freezer, so before you swing by the drive-through for bland egg muffins, consider these more flavorful options. Here are 23 of our favorite healthy breakfasts.

When it comes to healthy breakfast ideas, my mind immediately goes to oats. Also chia seed pudding. So the newest edition of my column Good for You is a chia-overnight oat hybrid, which also uses up a can of pumpkin purée (I bet you have one in the pantry.)

Frozen or fresh berries work great in these accidentally vegan pancakes (thanks, silken tofu!) from Good for You. A good drizzle of maple syrup is all they need for accompaniment, but since I’m not vegan, I enjoy them with a swipe of Greek yogurt.

Another Good for You hit is this speedy, protein- and collagen-rich soup, which is something in between Italian stracciatella and Chinese egg drop soup.

OK, after this one, I’ll stop shamelessly promoting my column. But seriously, don’t sleep on this hydrating mixed berry smoothie, especially if you’re lukewarm on eating plain cottage cheese.

This tricked out avocado toast is loaded with hot smoked salmon and creamy scrambled eggs.

You maybe haven’t seen an omelet filled with sliced apple and cheddar, but I recommend trying before knocking.

Oatmeal is an ideal choice in the morning for carbs and fiber. I like to slowly stir in egg whites while it’s simmering on the stove for extra protein and top it with ground flax seeds.

A great option for weekend mornings. You could top it with feta or yogurt, dunk whole wheat toast in the sauce or spoon it over rice, anything goes.

Nothing hits quite like a breakfast burrito, but I can’t buy one every day and still pay rent. DIY to the rescue: I line the tortilla with salsa or hot sauce, and sometimes add leftover refried beans if I have them.

The texture of chia seed pudding took me some time to get used to, but now I’m obsessed. Strange how the body adapts! I love that this recipe makes enough for a work week of breakfasts. Head here for our favorite matcha powders.

If you don’t have time to make the hot honey mixture, these simple breakfast tacos are great with any hot sauces or chile-based condiments that happen to be in the fridge.

This Persian egg dish is similar to a frittata, which means it makes for great meal prep. It takes a while to wash all the herbs, so make sure you have time, but leftovers will last a few days in the fridge. I like it cold with a swipe of Greek yogurt, but you do you.

Meet the bran muffins you’ll actually crave. Squidgy dates and chopped chocolate make these more like a hearty breakfast cake than the dense pucks you may have eaten in the past.

If it’s not heirloom tomato season, opt for smaller cherry or Campari tomatoes in this recipe—your taste buds will thank you.

To bulk up this green smoothie into a filling meal, add some nut butter and cottage cheese or plain yogurt, plus a scoop of protein powder if you have it.

Leftover cooked quinoa mocking you in the fridge? Stir it in to these blueberry muffins. Win, win.

Speaking of leftover quinoa, if you’re looking for a savory option instead of sweet, throw together this morning salad with a few boiled eggs and smoked trout.

While I personally love mushy porridge, I understand that cakey is a more pleasant texture for many. If that’s the case for you, baked oatmeal is the solution.

Serving brunch to a crowd? Make this breakfast casserole. Just grab a nice loaf of sourdough to slice alongside.

If you haven’t yet tried fried eggs that were based with turmeric oil, now is your moment.

For the savory oatmeal fans out there, try simmering with instant dashi powder and salt-softened vegetables.

A homemade gluten-free loaf that you’ll dream about. Try it with butter and a slice of aged cheese like cheddar, or cream cheese and smoked fish.

13 Best Deodorant for Men 2026, According to Dermatologists

📅 2017-06-11  |  Read Source →

Frankly, the best deodorant for men sells itself. It stinks to stink: Body odor is right up there with bad breath as a totally avoidable misfortune. But while deodorant might seem like an odd category to get overly psyched about, as a longtime grooming editor, I can confidently say we're living in a golden age of the underarm essential.

Unlike the synthetic and chalky ghosts of deodorant sticks past, today’s best deodorants are a a sea of sophisticated formulas, improved textures, better scents, and naturally derived ingredients that, yes, actually work. The only hard part is honing in on the best one for you. Price, feel, skin sensitivity, and your sweat and odor levels matter. So I tested a whole lot of deodorants to narrow the field to seven standouts, some new, some tried-and-true, plus a few more deeper cuts I also loved.

I also tapped trusted dermatologists, for some expert insights further down. Just one last quick note: We’re not talking antiperspirants here, which primarily rely on aluminum compounds to reduce sweat. If that’s what you’re after, I’ve got recommendations for those, too.

Now, are there more targeted, more sophisticated, and, well, chicer deodorants on this list? Absolutely. But at the end of the day, you want your deo to do three things: keep the stink at bay, smell good, and be easy to repurchase. Dove Men+Care hits every mark, which is why it's in every drugstore.

The price, of course, is great, but then there's the scent: a surprisingly sophisticated blend of bright bergamot and warm white amber that's fresh, subtly citrusy, and softly warm. It smells so much better than your typical underarm fare and is actually a little mood-boosting, as the packaging suggests. The aluminum-free, baking-soda-free formula features moisture-attracting ingredients, giving it a little skincare credibility, too. It also feels super smooth going on, and kept its scent for most of the day. No notes!

Hip grooming brand Church California's deodorant only launched two years ago, but for me, it's everything a niche, forward-thinking deodorant should be: Effective, made with almost entirely naturally derived ingredients, and housed in compostable, plastic-free packaging. The formula uses magnesium and organic tapioca starch for odor and moisture control, with coconut oil and shea butter adding a little skin-conditioning goodness. And that scent? Wonderfully herbaceous, thanks to an essential oil blend that smells like the actual outdoors.

Admittedly, the sturdy cardboard tube takes a little getting used to—you push the stick up from the bottom instead of twisting a dial. And the glide is certainly a little less smooth. But oh, does the formula hold up. Even hours later, I'd catch little whiffs of those lovely botanicals, and when the scent eventually faded, there was still no detectable B.O. For the environmentally conscious guy, it's a no-brainer, and for the aesthete, it looks retro cool sitting on your medicine cabinet shelf.

Ursa Major’s Hoppin’ Fresh stick is a modern legend in the deo space. Among us grooming geeks, this au naturel-meets-elevated formula changed the underarm game when it launched in 2015. It was one of those early artisanal step-ups from drugstore fare, and it earned the hype with thoughtfully curated, foraged-sounding ingredients, a subtle, natural-smelling citrus scent, and a skincare-centric stick that glides on smoothly.

It still holds up today, fighting odor, soothing skin, and absorbing excess moisture to keep you smelling fresh. For an aluminum-free deo, it also keeps me pretty dry, too. And even the packaging—a sleek, solid-blue tube—feels like a style upgrade. Scent wise, in winter Forest Fix is the way to go. But the original Hoppin' Fresh is always waiting in the wings once the warmer months come around.

I'm well aware that Old Spice has plenty of newer, more fragrance-centric variations, many with names like Swagger. But I've always preferred the tried-and-true stuff, and that blue stick, here in High Endurance Refresh, is about as iconic as deodorant gets. It's my old reliable, and one I always keep as a backup, whether I'm visiting family, staying overnight somewhere, or just need something I can count on in a pinch. This classic has aged remarkably well, because it simply works.

Then there's the nostalgia, which hits me every time I catch a whiff of that distinctively fresh-meets-medicinal aroma I've loved unironically for years. The formula isn't particularly fancy: science-y ingredients give the aluminum-free stick its smooth glide, while glycerin adds a little skin-conditioning benefit. Would I wear this for an Ironman competition? Probably not. But for everyday errands, workdays, and just about everything in between? Absolutely. At under $15 for a three-pack, it's also easily one of the best value options on this list.

When you think gel deodorant, you probably picture those old-school perforated dispensers with tons of holes. But Baxter of California's is a smooth, transparent gel stick that's still, in my book, among the best. Part of the appeal is the formula, which packs some impressive botanicals: witch hazel and tea tree oil help tackle odor-causing bacteria, while chamomile helps soothe skin. Add tried-and-true glycerin to the mix, and you've got a gel deodorant with some serious skincare cred, too.

Then there's the application itself. The clear gel glides on effortlessly, with a cool, crisp, almost medicinal feel that's especially refreshing during the warmer months. Better still, it won't leave white marks on your shirts, something I always appreciate with my go-to black T-shirts. Is it the most heavy-duty odor fighter on this list? Not quite. But for everyday wear, it keeps the funk at bay without feeling like you've applied much of anything.

I test a lot of deodorants, but few become permanent fixtures in my bathroom. So it's worth telling you that Freaks of Nature's Everyday Endurance has become my primary deodorant lately. The brand may be known best for sunscreen, but it knows its way around our underarms too. And much like its sunscreens, part of the appeal is the application. The lotion-like formula emerges through a clever F-shaped grate, and there's something oddly satisfying about watching it dispense before smoothing it onto your underarms.

Better still, it absorbs well without any gunky, sticky residue. Cocoa butter and sunflower oil give it a skin-conditioning quality that makes this one of the most soothing deodorants I've ever worn. It's premium-priced, sure, but between the standout application, exemplary performance, and skin-conditioning feel, it's worth every penny.

Gone are the days when deodorant had to smell like “Sports Ice Rush.” Today's best odor fighters offer sophisticated, subtle scents (so they don't compete with your cologne), and Salt & Stone remains one of the top examples, with deodorants that have actual scent pyramids—Saffron & Cedar, Black Rose & Oud, Bergamot & Hinoki, for example. All are nuanced, refined, and never too loud.

But it's not just about the scent. The formula neutralizes odor and absorbs excess moisture very well, while skincare-centric additions like niacinamide and shea butter help keep underarms comfortable. More importantly, in my experience, the fragrance doesn't devolve into some funky olfactory murk by day's end. You still smell like the scent you applied that morning, or at the very least, fresh. Are these the heaviest-duty or most affordable deodorants out there? Nope. But they deliver on fragrance and function, and that's a combination worth investing in.

Caldera Lab just launched its first deodorant, and given the brand's skincare chops, it's no surprise the formula reads like a serum: niacinamide, aloe vera, and hyaluronic acid soothe and hydrate; postbiotics and magnesium tackle odor; and arrowroot absorbs moisture. Stacked, indeed.

Can Aesop do any wrong? Not really, and its deodorant spray is another example of the botanical-meets-aromatic goodness the amber-hued brand does so well. It's the only spray on this list, and while its odor protection holds up well, a midday refresh is sometimes warranted—and honestly, part of the pleasure.

If your skin is extra sensitive, Nécessaire's entire fragrance-free lineup is exemplary, and its deodorant stick is no exception. It keeps odor impressively in check, is easy on sensitive underarms, and won't compete with your favorite cologne. It's also aluminum- and baking soda-free, dermatologist-tested, hypoallergenic, and even carries the National Eczema Association's Seal of Acceptance.

Cremo may be kings of shave cream, but it knows a thing or two about deodorant, too. The formulas are affordable, the odor protection holds up, and the scents smell so damn good. Take Palo Santo, with its warm, woody, slightly smoky aroma that feels far more sophisticated than its price suggests. Coconut oil and shea butter add the skin-conditioning touch you'd expect from a pricier stick. rant quietly in the background, look elsewhere—but if you like a lot of fragrance with your funk-fighting, Cremo delivers serious bang for the buck.

The Grey's culty, rightfully hyped deodorant is the best old-school-style roll-on I've ever tried. The unscented formula is so lightweight you almost have to check you're applying anything, but oh, you are, and there's a cooling tingle as it goes on. It's the most expensive deodorant on this list, but with its premium slim aluminum casing and impressively lightweight formula, it's an investment in barely-there odor-fighting care.

Malin+Goetz's Eucalyptus Deodorant is a stone-cold classic that helped elevate gel sticks and make them feel refreshingly natural. The scent is practically a trademark at this point, equal parts medicinal, herbal, and oh-so-subtle. But there's substance behind it: eucalyptus extract helps refresh skin, while odor-neutralizing citronellyl methylcrotonate keeps the funk at bay.

As a grooming editor, I've tried more sticks, sprays, gels, and roll-ons than I can count, from drugstore staples to niche newcomers. And while plenty have impressed me on first swipe, the real test is whether I keep reaching for them weeks, months, or even years later.

Here, I drew on firsthand testing, focusing on odor protection, scent, texture, application, skin comfort, and, of course, how well they hold up. I've worn these deodorants through warm and cool weather, long days, and everything in between. Some are favorites I've relied on for years, while others are newer that are now part of my medicine cabinet rotation. As I've said, the best deodorant sometimes depends on the occasion or even the season, so it's a good idea to have a few on hand if possible. At the end of the day, a deodorant needs to keep B.O. at bay, ideally absorb excess moisture, and feel comfortable. Some excel in certain areas more than others, but each one above warrants its spot among the best deodorants.

Here is some dermatologist advice on deodorants—the best ingredients to look for, plus some controversial ones, too—with insights from board-certified dermatologists Kiran Mian, Ryan B. Turner, and Heather Rogers.

The primary cause of body odor is “bad” bacteria pairing up with sweat. There are two ways to prevent the stink: Reduce perspiration in the area, and/or create an inhospitable environment for the bacteria.

These are the most common ingredients to seek out if you want to temper the proliferation of bacteria in your pits (and anywhere you’re particularly pungent).

This antimicrobial and anti-inflammatory mineral safely and effectively kills bacteria without irritating skin, says Mian.

Dr. Mian also endorses the non-irritating magnesium hydroxide, a naturally occurring compound that neutralizes bacteria without absorbing into the skin.

“The terpenes in tea tree oil make this ingredient naturally antibacterial and antifungal, helping to reduce body odor,” Turner says. “In its pure, concentrated form, it can be very irritating. It’s best to use a product that’s formulated with tea tree oil at low percentages. It’s still effective when diluted, and a little bit goes a very long way.”

This one is controversial to some. Most skin types will tolerate baking soda (sodium bicarbonate) just fine, but only trust reputable, well-reviewed brands who use it. “Baking soda has a very alkaline pH level, while that of the skin is more acidic,” explains Turner. “This disrupts the skin’s natural barrier, harming the good bacteria in our microbiome, and leaving our skin more susceptible to irritation and inflammation.” (We’ve included a few deodorants with baking soda below, plus many natural picks that explicitly exclude it.)

It’s easy to get these two confused, but they share a principle. “Prebiotics feed the good bacteria on our skin (bacteria that does not break down sweat releasing a foils odor), which then overpopulates those odor-producing bacteria,” explains Mian. “Probiotics are the good bacteria that will ideally populate the skin and overtake odor causing bacteria.” The names of these might be strange to you, like lactobacillus acidophilus or saccharomyces ferment. Chances are, a brand will proudly tout their pre- and probiotics, so look for that indication instead.

“These chemical exfoliating ingredients help resurface the skin, which also strips away bacteria as well as removes the dead skin that bacteria break down, which causes body odor,” says Turner. And Dr. Rogers echoes the love: “Using acids to exfoliate the skin in our armpits helps rebuild the acidic mantle [which is compromised with age], leading to better control of bacterial growth and odor. Many acids work great; glycolic, lactic acid and mandelic acid are my favorites. Salicylic acid, a beta hydroxy acid, has a higher chance of irritation.”

“Activated charcoal can help to absorb sweat and help to neutralize odors,” says Turner.

Arrowroot powder, tapioca starch, and cornstarch are popular ingredients in aluminum-free deodorants, helping absorb excess moisture and keep underarms feeling dry without blocking sweat production. Of tapioca starch, Mian says, “This non-irritating starch won’t clog sweat ducts like aluminum, which can cause breakouts.” (It's also terrific in anti-chafe products for similar moisture-absorbing benefits.) “Like tapioca starch, corn starch pulls moisture from the skin to leave a powdery feeling behind. Unless you have an allergy to these plants, they are safer to use,” says Turner. Both doctors particularly favor arrowroot powder as a naturally derived, generally well-tolerated ingredient for absorbing moisture.

Both help soak up surface moisture without blocking sweat production. Freaks of Nature's formula includes bentonite clay.

A naturally occurring, porous mineral powder that absorbs excess moisture and helps keep underarms dry. You'll find it in Ursa Major's deodorant.

Approach your deodorant purchase with the following considerations:

While it’s easy to celebrate a fully natural formula—and for good reason—keep in mind that not all natural ingredients are inherently good for your skin. Ingredients like baking soda and certain essential oils can cause irritation, particularly if your underarms are sensitive. Conversely, synthetic ingredients aren't automatically bad for your skin, either. “What’s more important is to consider the manufacturer of the products—are they reputable? Do they perform extensive testing? What are the ingredients used and from where are they sourced?” says Turner. That's sound advice whether you're shopping natural or synthetic. There's a reason dermatologists frequently recommend established brands like Dove: They have the resources to develop, test, and manufacture formulas at scale, often at a fraction of the price of more niche alternatives. Of course, that doesn't mean smaller brands aren't doing their homework, either. Finding the right deodorant still takes some trial and error, gents, but you can always start with our list.

If your underarms are easily irritated, start with the ingredient list. Baking soda, strong fragrances, essential oils, and certain acids can trigger irritation, though everyone's skin reacts differently. Look for skin-conditioning ingredients like shea butter, glycerin, and aloe, several of which you'll find in the deodorants featured above. Fragrance-free formulas are a smart starting point for sensitive skin, while unscented are worth considering, too—just know that some contain masking fragrances.

Deodorants come in all sorts of formats these days, from classic solid sticks and clear gels to sprays, roll-ons, lotions, and pastes. How they feel when you apply them matters as much as how well they work. Some glide on more smoothly, while others feel chalkier, thicker, or more moisturizing. Gels and roll-ons tend to feel lightweight and refreshing (great for warmer weather), while richer lotions and pastes feel more nourishing and suit the cooler months. Ultimately, the best deodorant feels comfortable, doesn't leave residue on your clothes, and fits your lifestyle.

Dr. Kiran Mian is a board-certified dermatologist at Hudson Dermatology and Laser Surgery in NYC, and volunteer faculty at the Icahn School of Medicine at Mount Sinai University.

Dr. Ryan B. Turner is a board-certified dermatologist and founder of Turner Dermatology in NYC and the TRNR Skin skin care line.

Dr. Heather Rogers is a board-certified dermatologist and co-founder of Modern Dermatology in Seattle and the founder of the Doctor Rogers skin care line.

The 16 Most Festive Cities in the US to Visit This Christmas Season

📅 2016-12-20  |  Read Source →

Even the most steadfast Scrooge will find themselves humming a holiday jingle in these places that pack on the seasonal cheer. The most festive cities in the US for Christmas are fully devoted to their merry-making, with multi-weekend celebrations, European-style holiday markets, millions of twinkling lights, and Christmas tree lighting ceremonies for the whole town to enjoy. Some tried and true classics appear on this list—the Big Apple, of course, is included—but others might come as a surprise. You’ll also find a city that celebrates the holidays in 18th-century style, a Danish town famous for its architecturally impressive gingerbread, and a ski destination that even sends Santa down the slopes. Below, tour the 15 most festive cities in the US for Christmas.

We couldn’t put together a Christmas list without giving a nod to NYC. The setting of many a holiday film, the Big Apple is full of Christmas spirit, including the Rockefeller Center tree lighting ceremony, Christmas shows like the Radio City Christmas Spectacular, the ice skating rinks in Central Park, and holiday windows at the iconic department stores.

Dog sledding, snowshoeing, skiing, and snowboarding are a major part of the holiday activities in this alpine city, which is home to the country’s largest lift-served ski resort, Park City Mountain. The resort’s Town Lift even carries Santa into Park City for an annual celebration with cocoa, carolers, and tunes. On Christmas Eve, Santa returns for a torchlight parade as over 100 ski and snowboard instructors carve their way down the mountain.

Can’t make it to Copenhagen before Christmas? Instead, head to Solvang—a deeply Danish town only about 35 miles from Santa Barbara that puts a Scandinavian spin on holiday cheer. The city’s seasonal celebration is called Julefest, which takes place from late November through early January and features holiday markets, a parade, carolers, and holiday concerts. Be sure to swing by The Solvang Bakery, famous for its elaborate gingerbread houses.

If you’re dreaming of a white Christmas, you’re pretty much guaranteed to see some snow in Vail, where alpine sports are in full swing by December. In addition to hitting the slopes at Vail Ski Resort, wandering its idyllic village—with cobblestone walkways, Bavarian-style architecture, and tons of twinkling lights—will make you feel like you’re in a real-life storybook. Take part in an ugly sweater fun run, do some holiday shopping at Kris Kringle Market, and take in a free skating show, featuring world-class athletes.

This tinsel-loving town in southern Indiana, about 60 miles from Louisville, Kentucky, celebrates Christmas year round. Holiday World, an amusement park with rides named after Santa’s reindeer; Santa’s Candy Castle, a confectionary shop that dates back to 1935; and the Santa Claus Christmas Store, stocked with 8,000 ornaments, are open beyond just December. But the last month of the year is when the holiday vibes kick into high gear. Over the first three weekends of December, the town hosts its annual Santa Claus Christmas Celebration, including a Christmas parade, an arts and crafts show, and elaborate holiday light displays. The city is also home to the Santa Claus Museum & Village, where local “elves” answer letters to Santa for kids from all over.

Despite its warm temperatures and palm trees instead of pines, Charleston curates a ridiculously cozy holiday scene. One beloved tradition is the Holiday Festival of Lights at James Island County Park, which includes a spectacle of more than 750 illuminated displays (even the palm trees are aglow), a holiday train ride, and visits with Santa. Elsewhere, historic homes are decked in vintage decor, and residential neighborhoods like I’On—just 15 minutes from downtown—go all in, with luminary-lined sidewalks, horse-drawn carriages, and neighbors gathering at the European-inspired wine bar Saint Urban’s for post-caroling sips.

The Windy City embraces the holidays with travel-worthy Christmas markets. The most famous is Christkindlmarket, a European-style outdoor extravaganza with handcrafted gifts, mulled wine, and giant German pretzels. On Michigan Avenue, an illuminated nighttime parade, The Wintrust Magnificent Mile Lights Festival, kicks off the season; an ice skating rink opens in Millennium Park; and hotels deck their halls in elaborately festive fashion. For example, The Pendry Chicago transforms its rooftop bar, Château Carbide, into a winter wonderland with cozy chalets, each featuring a personal fireplace.

Take a trip back in time at Colonial Williamsburg, which celebrates Christmas with 18th-century flair. While in town, make sure to visit the Busch Gardens Christmas Town, decorated with eight million lights.

Asheville’s iconic Biltmore Estate, built by George Vanderbilt, is America’s largest home. How fitting, then, that it features one of the grandest Christmas displays in the country. More than 70 trees fill the 175,000-square-foot house for the holiday season. In 2023, Hallmark even premiered a fantasy rom-com inspired by the spectacular, appropriately titled A Biltmore Christmas.

Experience an Ozark Mountain Christmas in Branson, the Live Music Show Capital of the World. (There are, of course, many special performances throughout the season.) Stroll down the two-and-a-half-mile Shepherd of the Hills Trail of Lights, or see the five million Christmas lights and 1,000 decorated trees at Silver Dollar City.

Starting December 20, this town hosts the 12 Days of Aspen, during which local businesses offer holiday activities to visitors and residents, from designing your own gift wrap at the St. Regis to a performance of Charles Dickens’s A Christmas Carol at the Wheeler Opera House.

From November to January, visit the Gilded Age mansions in Newport to see them filled with Christmas decorations. Plus, the whole town partakes in Christmas in Newport, a monthlong holiday celebration established in 1971, where residents and businesses alike decorate and host events.

The crown jewel of the Jersey Shore, this seaside town is filled with quaint bed and breakfasts and charming shops. The Victorian houses that line its streets are decorated with wreaths, lights, and garlands—take a ride on a holiday-themed trolley or join a popular walking tour to see them all.

Who says you need to have snow for Christmas? In Newport Beach, you can enjoy a 108-year-old tradition: the Newport Beach Christmas Boat Parade. More than a million people watch yachts, boats, and kayaks sail through the harbor each year. Outside of the annual spectacular, the parks services helps further set the holiday tone, adding Christmas trees in unexpected places, like at the Crystal Cove State Park, seen here.

BA’s Best Green Bean Casserole With Step-by-Step Video

📅 2016-11-11  |  Read Source →

This green bean casserole recipe skips the canned soup. Chris Morocco’s take on the classic Thanksgiving side pairs fresh green beans and deeply browned crimini mushrooms with a garlicky Parmesan béchamel. As for the topping, French’s fried onions are iconic for good reason, and they are staying right where they belong.

Give the mushrooms room to brown, salt the blanching water generously (like you would for cooking pasta), and taste the mushrooms and sauce before assembling. As written, you can cook the mushrooms and make the sauce at the same time. To save a pan, cook the mushrooms first, then transfer them to a plate and make the béchamel in the same pan, scraping up all the browned bits.

For the rest of the spread, turn to our classic Thanksgiving menu →

Can I make green bean casserole ahead of time?

Yes. Blanch the green beans, cook the mushrooms, and make the sauce up to 1 day ahead; cool and refrigerate them separately. Assemble and bake the day you plan to serve it, adding the fried onions during the final few minutes of baking.

We don’t recommend freezing the assembled casserole—the cream sauce can separate, and the beans turn mushy after thawing and reheating.

How do I keep the cream sauce from separating?

Don’t overreduce it. Once the béchamel is thick and bubbling, take it off the heat before whisking in the garlic and Parmesan. Boiling off too much liquid can cause the fat to separate. Stick with the recipe’s amount of Parmesan, too—extra cheese can contribute to a broken sauce.

Blanched, fresh beans are our first choice, but readers have reported success with frozen petite and French-cut beans. We haven’t tested the swap, but recommend thawing them overnight in your refrigerator and straining the excess liquid before continuing.

Can I make this green bean casserole gluten-free?

We haven’t tested a gluten-free version, but our editors suggest replacing the all-purpose flour with a gluten-free flour blend. You’ll also need to seek out gluten-free fried onions, or try our flourless Crispy Fried Shallots.

Place rack in middle of oven; preheat oven to 375°. Working in batches, cook 2 lb. fresh green beans in a large pot of boiling salted water until bright green and halfway to tenderness, about 3 minutes per batch. Drain and let cool; trim stem ends.

Heat 1 Tbsp. extra-virgin olive oil in a large skillet over medium-high. Cook ½ lb. crimini mushrooms, sliced, without stirring, until golden brown underneath, about 2 minutes. Toss and continue to cook, tossing occasionally, until browned on both sides, about 3 minutes more. Add 2 Tbsp. unsalted butter and 2 large thyme sprigs to pan. Cook, tossing occasionally, until butter browns and mushrooms are dark brown and very tender, about 4 minutes longer. Season with kosher salt and transfer to a plate. Repeat with 2 Tbsp. unsalted butter, salt, and remaining 1 Tbsp. extra-virgin olive oil, ½ lb. crimini mushrooms, sliced, and 2 large thyme sprigs.

Melt remaining 2 Tbsp. unsalted butter in a large saucepan over medium-low. Whisk in 2 Tbsp. all-purpose flour and cook, whisking occasionally, until roux is golden brown and smells mildly nutty, about 4 minutes. Whisk in 1¼ cups whole milk and 1 cup heavy cream. Increase heat and bring to a simmer, whisking often, and cook until béchamel sauce is thick and bubbling, about 5 minutes. Remove from heat and whisk in 4 garlic cloves, finely grated, and ½ cup grated Parmesan; season with salt and freshly ground pepper.

Arrange green beans and mushrooms in a 2-qt. baking dish. Pour sauce over bean mixture (it may not look like enough, but it will get saucier as it bakes). Cover tightly with foil and bake until sauce is bubbling, 25–30 minutes. Uncover and continue to bake until casserole is lightly browned on top and around the edges, 15–20 minutes longer. Top with ¾ cup French’s fried onions and continue to bake just until onions are slightly darker and fragrant, about 3 minutes. Let sit 10 minutes to set before serving.

Editor’s note: This green bean casserole recipe first appeared in November 2016 as part of BA’s Best, our vault of the Test Kitchen’s definitive takes on iconic dishes. Head this way for more of our go-to Thanksgiving recipes →

31 Thanksgiving Desserts to Cap Your Holiday Feast

There’s Thanksgiving dinner, and then there are Thanksgiving desserts. These are two separate events, and in between, it’s perfectly fine to take a disco nap. (In fact, we recommend it.)

“This is the magnum opus of Thanksgiving dessert recipes,” writes one reader whose 90-year-old grandfather asked for a repeat performance. “You bet your boots I will—it’s the best apple pie, ever.”

A colossal, self-serve mousse is a baller host move—no tidy slicing, no fussy garnishing, no jockeying for oven space. Set out bowls and spoons and let guests help themselves. Vanilla lovers, try our Colossal Crème Brûlée.

Cinnamon-spiced pumpkin cake rolled around walnut-studded cream cheese filling needs little embellishment. A dusting of powdered sugar is plenty—each slice supplies its own dramatic swirl.

This glamorous layer cake, with jewel-toned swipes of cranberry jam, white chocolate–cream cheese frosting, and sugared cranberries, looks and tastes like winter is coming (in the best possible way).

Inspired by the flavors of a pumpkin spice latte, this rendition of old-school tiramisu calls for soaking ladyfingers in bourbon-spiked espresso, then enveloping them in plumes of pumpkin-mascarpone cream.

Cranberries with dinner and again for dessert? Absolutely. Tart filling hides between layers of buttery, crumbly walnut crust in this Linzer tart. Prefer individual portions? Try these cranberry hand pies.

Pumpkin crumb cake is like the brownie of Thanksgiving desserts: easy to tote and slice for sharing. Here, sweet rice flour gives the cake a chewy bounce beneath a buttery, crispy-crumbly pecan streusel.

The ultraflaky crust barely contains the lofty, fudgy almond filling, but have faith: It will puff and dome as it bakes, then settle back into sliceable form once cool.

Alternate layers of pumpkin and chocolate batter create this Bundt cake’s mesmerizing marbling—no knife-swirling necessary. A blanket of chocolate ganache makes it equally appealing to the pumpkin crowd and the chocolate diehards.

Whole roasted birds, spatchcocked showstoppers, braised legs, breasts for smaller gatherings, and more ways to put turkey at the center of the table.
